India crosses over 80 lakh HPV vaccinations in its fight against cervical cancer - The New Indian Express

News Brief
India has administered over 80 lakh doses of HPV vaccine under the National HPV Vaccination Campaign to prevent cervical cancer among adolescent girls. The campaign has achieved 100% coverage in some states and is expanding access to HPV vaccination in diverse areas, including rural and remote regions. The Ministry of Health and Family Welfare continues to work closely with states and healthcare professionals to ensure no girl is left behind.
Original source text (English)
Dinamani Kannada Prabha Samakalika Malayalam Cinema Express Indulgexpress Edexlive Sep 11, 2026 9:53 PM Subscribe E-PAPER INDIA WORLD STATES CITIES OPINION EXPLAINER BUSINESS SPORTS GOOD NEWS ENTERTAINMENT VIDEOS LIVE BLOG INDIA WORLD STATES CITIES OPINION EXPLAINER BUSINESS SPORTS GOOD NEWS ENTERTAINMENT VIDEOS LIVE BLOG Trending Links Speaker J C D Prabhakar TVK Government Tamil Nadu Legislative Assembly Sanjay Jha Vinesh Phogat Advertisement India India crosses over 80 lakh HPV vaccinations in its fight against cervical cancer Under the campaign, 14-year-old girls are provided voluntary and free-of-cost single-dose vaccination against human papillomavirus (HPV), an infection that causes cervical cancer, which is the second most common cancer among women in the country. Among states, Uttar Pradesh has recorded the highest number of vaccinations, with more than 22 lakh adolescent girls vaccinated under the campaign.(Photo | ANI) Kavita Bajeli-Datt Updated on: 11 Sep 2026, 1:11 pm 3 min read Copied NEW DELHI: In its efforts to prevent cervical cancer, India has administered over 80 lakh doses of Human Papillomavirus (HPV) vaccine under the National HPV Vaccination Campaign over the past six months, officials said Friday. Gujarat, Uttar Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh and Mizoram have achieved 100 per cent coverage of their respective identified target populations, while Bihar, Andhra Pradesh and Assam have crossed 90 per cent coverage, officials added. While Karnataka has achieved more than 85 per cent coverage, Chhattisgarh, Sikkim, Kerala, Telangana and Odisha have crossed 60 per cent coverage, they added. Among states, Uttar Pradesh has recorded the highest number of vaccinations, with more than 22 lakh adolescent girls vaccinated under the campaign. “The milestone marks the expanding reach of HPV vaccination through the public health system and reinforces India’s commitment to strengthening preventive healthcare for adolescent girls,” the officials added. The National HPV Vaccination Campaign was launched by Prime Minister Narendra Modi on February 28. Under the campaign, 14-year-old girls are provided voluntary and free-of-cost single-dose vaccination against human papillomavirus (HPV), an infection that causes cervical cancer, which is the second most common cancer among women in the country. The vaccination is given with parental consent at government health facilities. All vaccination sessions under the campaign are conducted under the supervision of trained medical officers. “Government health facilities are also linked with 24×7 healthcare facilities...following Immunization (AEFI). This mechanism ensures timely assessment, monitoring and management of any vaccination-related adverse event,” mentioned a statement from the Union Health Ministry. As per Registrar General of India (RGI) 2021 estimates, approximately 1.2 crore girls constitute the annual cohort of 14-year-olds who are expected to benefit from the initiative each year. Andhra Pradesh records 98.47 per cent HPV coverage As many states have made significant progress in expanding HPV vaccination coverage, the officials said this “progress reflects coordinated implementation by states and union territories, sustained community mobilisation and active engagement of district-level health teams.” States have adopted context-specific approaches to reach eligible adolescent girls through the public health delivery network, including in rural, urban, remote and geographically challenging areas, they said. Officials pointed out that MP and Gujarat have achieved 100 per cent coverage of their identified target cohorts, supported by sustained community mobilisation, coordinated implementation and focused field-level efforts. The progress in Assam and other northeastern states also demonstrates the role of integrated community health networks and coordinated field-level action in expanding access across geographically diverse areas. The campaign has placed strong emphasis on community engagement and credible information, with parents, healthcare providers, teachers and frontline health workers being actively engaged to address concerns and counter misinformation relating to HPV vaccination. Healthcare providers, including paediatricians, gynaecologists and experts from institutions such as AIIMS, along with teachers and frontline health workers including ASHAs and ANMs, have supported awareness and mobilisation efforts across states and districts. “The campaign has also leveraged India’s digital health infrastructure through the U-WIN platform, enabling programme managers to monitor vaccination coverage at the district level, identify coverage gaps and missed beneficiaries, and support more effective programme planning and resource allocation,” the statement said. “The national campaign builds on extensive global experience with HPV vaccination,” it added. More than 800 million doses of HPV vaccines have been distributed globally, providing substantial real-world experience on their use and safety. HPV vaccination, initially adopted predominantly by high-income countries, has progressively expanded into national immunisation programmes across countries at different income levels. At present, 87 per cent of upper-middle-income countries, 78 per cent of lower-middle-income countries and 54 per cent of low-income countries have adopted HPV vaccines in their National Immunization Programmes. The growing adoption of HPV vaccination across diverse income settings reflects its increasing role as an important component of national strategies for cervical cancer prevention, the statement said. With the campaign progressing towards its final phase, the focus remains on reaching the remaining eligible beneficiaries and ensuring that no eligible girl is left behind. “The Ministry of Health and Family Welfare continues to work closely with states and union territories, healthcare professionals, frontline health workers and communities to strengthen awareness, facilitate access and support effective implementation of the HPV Vaccination Campaign,” the statement said. The statement added that achievement of 80 lakh HPV vaccine doses marks an important step in India’s efforts to strengthen preventive healthcare and advance long-term protection against cervical cancer among future generations. According to the World Health Organisation (WHO), cervical cancer is the fifth most commonly diagnosed cancer in women globally with around 6,04,000 new cases and around 2,80,000 deaths in 2024. It is largely preventable through HPV vaccination and regular screening and it can be cured if detected early and treated promptly. India reports over 1,20,000 new cases and nearly 80,000 fatalities from cervical cancer each year, according to the 2022 WHO GLOBOCAN report. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

印度 HPV 患者突破 80 万例,在 宫颈癌 预防行动中 - newsdrum.in

India crosses 80 lakh HPV vaccinations in cervical cancer prevention drive - newsdrum.in
新闻简报India's national HPV vaccination drive has surpassed 8 million doses, a milestone announced on 11 September 2026. The free, single‐dose campaign, launched by Prime Minister Narendra Modi on 28 February 2026, aims to immunise around 12 million 14‐year‐old girls across all states and Union Territories. Administered through government health facilities and tracked via the U‐WIN digital platform, the effort is part of a broader cervical‐cancer prevention strategy that also includes screening of over 87 million women. States such as Gujarat, Uttar Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh and Mizoram have achieved full coverage of their target cohorts. By reaching this scale, the programme seeks to curb a disease that causes more than 120 000 new cases and 80 000 deaths each year in India, though the initiative remains ongoing with no reported safety issues.
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章于 2026 年 9 月 11 日(印度标准时间 16:37)发布。该 疫苗接种 里程碑于同一天报告,而全国 HPV 接种计划则于 2026 年 2 月 28 日启动。
地点与对象
印度,报告来自新德里。该计划旨在覆盖全国约1.2 crore(12万)14岁女孩,如古吉拉特邦、北方邦、中央邦和米佐拉姆邦等州已实现全覆盖。
事件
The government announced that more than 80 lakh (8 million) doses of the human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccine have been administered under the free, single‐dose national drive. The milestone was communicated by National Health India and reported by the news outlet.
规模、影响与风险
Over 8 million adolescent girls have received the vaccine, representing a substantial step toward reducing cervical cancer, which accounts for over 1.2 lakh new cases and about 80 000 deaths annually in India. The campaign is part of a broader strategy that includes screening of 8.73 crore women.
应对、状态与不确定性
该 疫苗接种 接种计划是自愿的、免费的,并通过政府卫生设施、阿亚ushman 健康中心、初级和社区卫生中心以及医院进行。接种记录通过 U‐WIN 数字平台进行追踪,而 疫苗 的物流则通过 eVIN 进行管理。该计划仍在继续,包括持续的筛查和 治疗 组件;未报告任何未解决的安全问题。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India crosses 80 lakh HPV vaccinations in cervical cancer prevention drive - newsdrum.in

News Brief
India's national HPV vaccination drive has surpassed 8 million doses, a milestone announced on 11 September 2026. The free, single‐dose campaign, launched by Prime Minister Narendra Modi on 28 February 2026, aims to immunise around 12 million 14‐year‐old girls across all states and Union Territories. Administered through government health facilities and tracked via the U‐WIN digital platform, the effort is part of a broader cervical‐cancer prevention strategy that also includes screening of over 87 million women. States such as Gujarat, Uttar Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh and Mizoram have achieved full coverage of their target cohorts. By reaching this scale, the programme seeks to curb a disease that causes more than 120 000 new cases and 80 000 deaths each year in India, though the initiative remains ongoing with no reported safety issues.
Original source text (English)
National Health India crosses 80 lakh HPV vaccinations in cervical cancer prevention drive The free single-dose drive targets 14-year-old girls, while screening, early diagnosis and treatment form the government's wider prevention strategy Akansha Srivastava 11 Sep 2026 16:37 IST New Update File image of Prime Minister Narendra Modi overseeing the dose of HPV vaccine being given to a girl child. New Delhi: India has administered more than 80 lakh doses of the Human Papillomavirus (HPV) vaccine under its nationwide vaccination campaign, crossing a major milestone in efforts to reduce the country's cervical cancer burden. Advertisment Add NewsDrum As A Trusted Source The National HPV Vaccination Campaign was launched by Prime Minister Narendra Modi on February 28, 2026, with the government targeting around 1.2 crore girls aged 14 years across all states and Union Territories. Under the programme, eligible girls are offered a voluntary, single dose of the HPV vaccine free of cost at designated government health facilities after parental consent. The vaccine is available through Ayushman Arogya Mandirs, primary and community health centres, district and sub-district hospitals and government medical colleges. The crossing of the 80-lakh mark is significant because cervical cancer remains one of the biggest cancer-related health challenges facing Indian women. According to GLOBOCAN 2022 figures cited by the government, cervical cancer is the second most common cancer among women in India, with more than 1.2 lakh new cases and nearly 80,000 deaths annually. Persistent infection with high-risk types of HPV is responsible for almost all cervical cancers. HPV types 16 and 18 account for a large majority of cervical cancer cases in India. Why is HPV vaccination important? HPV is a common virus transmitted primarily through sexual contact. While most infections clear naturally, persistent infection with certain high-risk HPV types can cause changes in cervical cells that may eventually develop into cancer. This is also why vaccination is targeted at adolescent girls before likely exposure to the virus. The World Health Organisation recommends HPV vaccination primarily for girls aged 9 to 14 years and considers vaccination before age 15 a cornerstone of its strategy to eliminate cervical cancer as a public health problem. India's programme uses the quadrivalent Gardasil-4 vaccine, which protects against HPV types 16 and 18 as well as types 6 and 11. The government's backgrounder says a single dose provides 93-100 per cent effectiveness against cervical cancer caused by the HPV types covered by the vaccine. WHO also permits a single-dose HPV vaccination schedule for people aged 9 to 20 years, a policy that can make large-scale programmes easier and less expensive to implement while improving access. How is the government programme being carried out? The nationwide programme began with an intensive vaccination drive and is being delivered through India's public immunisation infrastructure. After the initial intensive phase, the vaccine was to remain available on routine immunisation days at government health facilities. Vaccination records are maintained through the U-WIN digital platform, while vaccine stocks and logistics are monitored through eVIN. The vaccination drive is only one part of the government's broader cervical cancer strategy. The Centre has said it is simultaneously pursuing screening and early diagnosis through Ayushman Arogya Mandirs and treatment through Day Care Cancer Centres. As of February 17, 2026, more than 8.73 crore women had been screened for cervical cancer through the public health system, according to government data. This multi-pronged approach is important because vaccination protects younger generations against future disease but cannot by itself address infections or cervical abnormalities already present among adult women. Screening and timely treatment therefore remain necessary alongside vaccination. WHO's global cervical cancer elimination strategy similarly calls for 90 per cent of girls to be fully vaccinated against HPV by age 15, 70 per cent of women to undergo appropriate screening by ages 35 and 45, and 90 per cent of women diagnosed with cervical disease to receive treatment by 2030. Which states are leading? According to the latest government release, Gujarat, Uttar Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh and Mizoram have achieved 100 per cent coverage of their identified target cohorts. Bihar, Andhra Pradesh and Assam have crossed 90 per cent coverage, while Karnataka has vaccinated more than 85 per cent of its target group. Chhattisgarh, Sikkim, Kerala, Telangana and Odisha have crossed the 60 per cent mark. In absolute numbers, Uttar Pradesh has administered the highest number of HPV vaccinations, with more than 22 lakh adolescent girls covered so far. With an estimated annual cohort of around 1.2 crore 14-year-old girls, maintaining high coverage every year, while improving screening among adult women, will be crucial if India is to translate the vaccination milestone into a sustained fall in cervical cancer cases and deaths over the coming decades. The article was published on 11 September 2026 (16:37 IST). The vaccination milestone was reported on the same date, while the national HPV campaign was launched on 28 February 2026.

India, with the report originating from New Delhi. The programme targets approximately 1.2 crore (12 million) 14‐year‐old girls nationwide, with states such as Gujarat, Uttar Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh and Mizoram achieving full coverage.
Event
The government announced that more than 80 lakh (8 million) doses of the human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccine have been administered under the free, single‐dose national drive. The milestone was communicated by National Health India and reported by the news outlet.
Scale, impact and risk
Over 8 million adolescent girls have received the vaccine, representing a substantial step toward reducing cervical cancer, which accounts for over 1.2 lakh new cases and about 80 000 deaths annually in India. The campaign is part of a broader strategy that includes screening of 8.73 crore women.
Response, status and uncertainty
The vaccination is voluntary, free of charge, and delivered through government health facilities, Ayushman Arogya Mandirs, primary and community health centres, and hospitals. Records are tracked via the U‐WIN digital platform and vaccine logistics via eVIN. The programme continues, with ongoing screening and treatment components; no unresolved safety concerns were reported.
